He used to love going up to the Adirondacks to fish, but I never saw the appeal of sitting in a damp boat at five in the morning.\n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Mistaken? No, absolutely not. Even when everyone says it's just mistakes or coincidences, I know the truth. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's perfectly clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about these people trying to keep information from you when you're not directly dealing with your sister's appointments or bills?\n\nPatient: Think about them all the time? Well, people might think I'm too focused on this whole situation, but no. Outside of dealing with my sister's appointments and trying to get straight answers from these people, I still keep my home in perfect order, I pay my bills, and I do my job. \n\nSpeaking of doing my job, we had an absolute disaster at the office on Tuesday. One of the new administrative assistants—sweet girl, but absolutely no common sense—tried to replace the toner in the main copier without reading the manual first. Toner exploded everywhere. It looked like a coal mine in the breakroom! And it got all over my favorite cream-colored silk blouse. You can't just wash silk, you know. You have to take it to a specialty dry cleaner. I use this place downtown, run by a lovely couple, but they just raised their prices again. It's getting ridiculous, really, but what can you do? If you try to hand-wash silk yourself, it loses that beautiful sheen and just looks like cheap polyester. I bought that blouse for my niece's college graduation back in 2019. She got her degree in communications, whatever that means nowadays. She wants to do public relations, but honestly, half the time she's just staring at her phone making those little videos. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, about thinking about it when I'm not dealing with it. No. Like I said, I’m not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I go to work, I manage my staff, I keep my house clean. I just know what's going on with my sister's care, and I won't let them sweep it under the rug.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself questioning your own thoughts about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Question my own thoughts? No, why would I? I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It's not something I'm making up in my head. My sister’s appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of bills showing up wrong, the electric company tried to charge me double last month! They said it was an estimated reading because the meter reader couldn't get into the yard. But my gate is never locked. I have one of those wrought iron gates, very classic looking. I had it installed after the neighborhood association complained about my old wooden fence leaning too far over the sidewalk. Some people just have nothing better to do than measure the angle of a fence, I swear. The woman who heads the committee, Shirley, she walks her little Pomeranian past my house every morning at exactly 7:15. You can set your watch by it. She always wears these bright pink tracksuits. Who wears bright pink at seven in the morning? It's just entirely too loud for that time of day. I prefer a nice muted beige or a soft navy. Navy is so professional, don't you think? It commands respect without being aggressive like red. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Questioning myself? No, no. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or clerical errors, I know exactly what's happening. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any circumstance under which you would consider the possibility that these events are not part of a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Consider the possibility? No, why would I do that when the proof is right in front of me? I've been seeing the exact same pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, the plumber I called last week still hasn't returned my message. I have a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom, and it's driving me absolutely up the wall. Drip, drip, drip. It's one of those antique brass fixtures, too, so you can't just go to the hardware store and buy a standard washer for it. You have to order the parts special. I found them online, but the shipping takes forever. I hate ordering things online. The delivery drivers always leave the packages right on the edge of the porch where anyone walking by could just snatch them up. Last Christmas, someone stole a fruitcake I had ordered for my uncle. Not that he would have eaten it anyway—he has terrible dentures and can't chew anything harder than oatmeal. He got those dentures fitted back in 2012, and he's complained about them every single day since. He always says they make him look like a horse when he smiles. \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Circumstances? No, there are no circumstances where I'd think it's just mistakes or coincidences. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you ever consider seeking a second opinion about what's happening with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Second opinion? From who? Another doctor who's just going to cover for the rest of them? I don't need a second opinion because I already know what's happening. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of doctors covering for each other, my dentist tried to refer me to an endodontist last month for a root canal. He said my molar was cracked, but I don't feel any pain at all. None. I think they just send patients back and forth to drum up business for each other. It's like those mechanics who tell you that you need new brake pads when you just went in for an oil change. I take my car to a guy named Sal over on 4th Street. He's honest, mostly, but his waiting room smells strongly of old cigars and wet dog. He has this massive mastiff that just sleeps right in the middle of the floor, and you have to step over him to get to the counter. The dog's name is Brutus, which is so cliché, don't you think? I always thought if I got a dog, I'd name it something elegant, like Winston or maybe Oliver. But I don't have the time for a pet. They tie you down too much. You can't just take a weekend trip to the coast if you have to worry about who is going to feed the dog. The coast is lovely this time of year, though. The sea air is so bracing. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? A second opinion? No, I don't need one. Even when everyone—the nurses, the clinic manager, everyone—says it’s just mistakes, I know better. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's obvious.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could happen or anything someone could tell you that would make you change your mind about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? What could possibly happen to change my mind when the facts are right there? I've been seeing this exact pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without a word to me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of not calling back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she never calls me back either. I called her three times last week to ask if she’d seen anyone lingering around my sister’s porch, because I noticed the potted geraniums were moved. Geraniums are so finicky, you know. If they don't get exactly the right amount of morning sun, they just wither up. I used to have a whole row of them on my balcony when I lived in the city, back when I was managing that big insurance office downtown. I oversaw a team of twenty administrative assistants. Twenty! You really have to know how to manage people to keep a place like that running. Most of them were fresh out of college and didn't know the first thing about filing or proper phone etiquette. I had to write a whole manual on how to answer the phone. \"Good morning, Smith and Associates, how may I direct your call?\" It's not hard, but you'd be surprised. One girl, Brenda, she used to chew gum while she answered the phones. Smacked it right in the receiver. I had to let her go after a month. You just can't have that kind of unprofessionalism when you're dealing with important clients. \n\nBut what were you asking? Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the billing department—they all try to tell me it's just mistakes or clerical errors. But I know better. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. Nothing is going to change my mind because I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would change your belief about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could they possibly have? I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of evidence, I was watching this true crime documentary last night on cable. They were talking about forensic evidence, DNA and all that. It's fascinating how they can solve a crime from thirty years ago just from a single strand of hair. I always thought I'd be good at that kind of work, paying attention to all the little details. You really need an eye for detail. Like when I was auditing the expense reports at the insurance firm. I caught one of the junior executives trying to expense a $400 dinner at a steakhouse as a \"client meeting\" when it was clearly his anniversary dinner. He had ordered a bottle of champagne! Who orders champagne at a client dinner on a Tuesday? I flagged it immediately. My supervisor, Mr. Henderson, was so impressed. Mr. Henderson was a good man, but he wore these terrible knit ties that were always slightly crooked. I wanted to reach across his desk and straighten them every time we had a meeting. He retired to Florida a few years ago. Bought a condo in Boca Raton. I hear the humidity down there is just awful for your joints. My knees ache just thinking about it. \n\nBut what were you asking? Evidence? No, there's no evidence that would change my mind. Even when everyone—the clinic manager, the doctors, the billing staff—even when they all look me in the eye and say it's just administrative mistakes, I know what's really going on. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's clear as day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doubt it? Doctor, I feel like a broken record today. Like I keep saying, I have watched this exact pattern for months. The appointments getting shuffled around behind my back, the billing errors, the staff suddenly forgetting how to dial my phone number... everyone keeps telling me they're just innocent mistakes until they're blue in the face, but I know exactly what's going on. They are deliberately keeping information from me to push me out of the decisions about my sister's care. 

Speaking of blue in the face, that reminds me of this horrible blue carpeting corporate made us install at the insurance office back in 2004. They claimed it would create a "calming work environment," but it was this cheap, awful shag material. It was a complete nightmare to keep clean. The girls' rolling chairs would just sink right into it. I had to order special plastic floor mats for all twenty of my administrative assistants just so they could roll from their desks to the filing cabinets without getting stuck. 

And getting those mats approved by the purchasing department? Don't even get me started. The purchasing manager, Greg, guarded the office supply budget like it was his own personal fortune. He would literally count the boxes of paperclips. I told him once, "Greg, we are a major firm, we can afford the heavy-duty binder clips." He was a very strange man. He drove this tiny little yellow car that looked like a lemon on wheels. I actually ran into him at the grocery store once on a Sunday, and he had six bags of frozen peas in his cart. Just peas. Who eats that many frozen peas? It’s terrible for your digestion to eat that much starch. 

But... wait, what did you ask me? Something small? No, absolutely nothing is going to make me doubt it. I know they're keeping me in the dark about my sister. It's not just mistakes.

My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, the plumber I called last week still hasn't returned my message. Circumstances? No, there are no circumstances where I'd think it's just mistakes or coincidences. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you ever consider seeking a second opinion about what's happening with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Second opinion? From who? Another doctor who's just going to cover for the rest of them? I don't need a second opinion because I already know what's happening. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of doctors covering for each other, my dentist tried to refer me to an endodontist last month for a root canal. You can't just take a weekend trip to the coast if you have to worry about who is going to feed the dog. The coast is lovely this time of year, though. The sea air is so bracing. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? A second opinion? No, I don't need one. Even when everyone—the nurses, the clinic manager, everyone—says it’s just mistakes, I know better. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's obvious.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could happen or anything someone could tell you that would make you change your mind about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? My sister's appointments get moved without a word to me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of not calling back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she never calls me back either. I called her three times last week to ask if she’d seen anyone lingering around my sister’s porch, because I noticed the potted geraniums were moved. Geraniums are so finicky, you know. If they don't get exactly the right amount of morning sun, they just wither up. I used to have a whole row of them on my balcony when I lived in the city, back when I was managing that big insurance office downtown. I oversaw a team of twenty administrative assistants. Twenty! You really have to know how to manage people to keep a place like that running. Most of them were fresh out of college and didn't know the first thing about filing or proper phone etiquette. I had to write a whole manual on how to answer the phone. \"Good morning, Smith and Associates, how may I direct your call?\" It's not hard, but you'd be surprised. One girl, Brenda, she used to chew gum while she answered the phones. Smacked it right in the receiver. I had to let her go after a month. You just can't have that kind of unprofessionalism when you're dealing with important clients. \n\nBut what were you asking? Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the billing department—they all try to tell me it's just mistakes or clerical errors. But I know better. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. Nothing is going to change my mind because I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would change your belief about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could they possibly have? I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of evidence, I was watching this true crime documentary last night on cable. They were talking about forensic evidence, DNA and all that. It's fascinating how they can solve a crime from thirty years ago just from a single strand of hair. I always thought I'd be good at that kind of work, paying attention to all the little details. You really need an eye for detail. Like when I was auditing the expense reports at the insurance firm. I caught one of the junior executives trying to expense a $400 dinner at a steakhouse as a \"client meeting\" when it was clearly his anniversary dinner. He had ordered a bottle of champagne! Who orders champagne at a client dinner on a Tuesday? I flagged it immediately. My supervisor, Mr. Henderson, was so impressed. Mr. Henderson was a good man, but he wore these terrible knit ties that were always slightly crooked. I wanted to reach across his desk and straighten them every time we had a meeting. He retired to Florida a few years ago. Bought a condo in Boca Raton. I hear the humidity down there is just awful for your joints. My knees ache just thinking about it. \n\nBut what were you asking? Evidence? No, there's no evidence that would change my mind. Even when everyone—the clinic manager, the doctors, the billing staff—even when they all look me in the eye and say it's just administrative mistakes, I know what's really going on. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's clear as day.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything, no matter how small, that could make you doubt your belief that people are intentionally keeping information from you?\n\nPatient: Doubt it? Doctor, I feel like a broken record today. Like I keep saying, I have watched this exact pattern for months. The appointments getting shuffled around behind my back, the billing errors, the staff suddenly forgetting how to dial my phone number... everyone keeps telling me they're just innocent mistakes until they're blue in the face, but I know exactly what's going on. They are deliberately keeping information from me to push me out of the decisions about my sister's care. \n\nSpeaking of blue in the face, that reminds me of this horrible blue carpeting corporate made us install at the insurance office back in 2004. They claimed it would create a \"calming work environment,\" but it was this cheap, awful shag material. It was a complete nightmare to keep clean. The girls' rolling chairs would just sink right into it. I had to order special plastic floor mats for all twenty of my administrative assistants just so they could roll from their desks to the filing cabinets without getting stuck. \n\nAnd getting those mats approved by the purchasing department? Don't even get me started. The purchasing manager, Greg, guarded the office supply budget like it was his own personal fortune. He would literally count the boxes of paperclips. I told him once, \"Greg, we are a major firm, we can afford the heavy-duty binder clips.\" He was a very strange man. He drove this tiny little yellow car that looked like a lemon on wheels. I actually ran into him at the grocery store once on a Sunday, and he had six bags of frozen peas in his cart. Just peas. Who eats that many frozen peas? It’s terrible for your digestion to eat that much starch. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? Something small? No, absolutely nothing is going to make me doubt it. I know they're keeping me in the dark about my sister. It's not just mistakes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I still have to see a chiropractor twice a month.\n\nBut anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, if I think about them all the time. No, outside of dealing with my sister’s appointments and just trying to get a straight answer out of those schedulers, I function perfectly normally. I have a very full schedule, so I'm not just sitting around obsessing all day, even if I know exactly what they're trying to pull.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you personally, or is it just about keeping information from you?\n\nPatient: Harm me personally? No, I don't think they're trying to hurt me, like physically or anything like that. It's really just about keeping the information from me and pushing me out of the loop with my sister's care. They just want me out of the way. \n\nSpeaking of being out of the way, I had to completely rearrange the breakroom at the office yesterday because people kept leaving their dirty coffee mugs right in the sink instead of putting them in the dishwasher. It’s not that hard! The dishwasher is literally two feet to the left. I had to type up a very clear, bolded sign and tape it right above the faucet. You know, I used to use that heavy-duty packing tape for signs, but it leaves this awful sticky residue on the wall. I found this wonderful double-sided mounting tape at Staples last week. It holds up beautifully and peels right off. I bought three rolls just to have in my supply closet. The cashier at Staples was so slow, though. He had to call a manager over just to process a simple coupon I had from their email newsletter. I stood there for ten minutes. I could have audited a whole stack of invoices in the time it took him to scan a barcode. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, they aren't trying to harm me. They just want me in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Wrong? No. I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It's clear as day. My sister’s appointments get moved without my permission, bills show up completely wrong, and people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, my mechanic has had my Subaru since Tuesday for a simple brake pad replacement. He said he'd call me by noon yesterday, and nothing! I finally had to call him myself at four o'clock, and he tried to tell me they were waiting on a part to be delivered. A brake pad! For a 2018 Outback! They are the most common cars on the road, especially around here. You can't throw a rock without hitting a silver Subaru Outback. I bought mine in silver specifically because it doesn't show the salt spray in the winter. They use so much road salt in this county, it's terrible for the undercarriage. I have to take it to the car wash on Main Street at least once a week from December to March. The guy who runs the wash, Hector, he always tries to upsell me on the premium wax treatment. I tell him, \"Hector, I just want the salt off, I don't need a showroom shine.\" He's a nice man, though. His daughter just graduated from the state college with a degree in communications. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, about being wrong. No, even when everyone tells me it’s just mistakes or short-staffing, I know the truth. I know they’re deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's not a mistake, it's a strategy.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind if you found out that the mistakes with your sister's appointments and bills were actually due to clerical errors and not a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Because I'm here because for months now, I've been seeing the exact same pattern. It's not just one or two little clerical errors. My sister's appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of bills showing up wrong, I just got my credit card statement yesterday and there was a charge from a restaurant I haven't been to in three years. Three years! It was that little Italian place over on 4th Street, Mario's. I stopped going there because they changed their marinara sauce recipe. It used to be so rich and garlicky, and then suddenly it tasted like it came straight out of a tin can. I asked the waiter about it—he was a young kid, couldn't have been more than nineteen, had his hair slicked back with way too much gel—and he swore nothing changed. But I know my palate. I used to cook all the time before I got promoted to office supervisor. I made a lasagna that won first prize at the church potluck back in 2012. I used three different kinds of cheese and homemade pasta. Making pasta from scratch takes forever, though. You have to let the dough rest for at least thirty minutes, and then rolling it out is an absolute workout for your shoulders. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, about changing my mind. No. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or short-staffing, I know the truth. I know they’re deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. You don't just have this many \"clerical errors\" by coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that these events are not part of a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Convince me? No, I don't think so. I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It’s right there in front of everyone. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting things, I was at the grocery store this morning—the big one over on Elm, not the little market by my house—and I completely forgot my reusable bags. I had them sitting right by the front door, right next to the umbrella stand. I bought that umbrella stand at a flea market out in Lancaster County about ten years ago. It’s real brass, very heavy. You don't see craftsmanship like that anymore. Everything now is just cheap plastic from overseas. I tried to buy a new watering can for my African violets last week, and the handle snapped off the very first time I filled it. African violets are so finicky, you know. You can't get water on their leaves or they rot. You have to water them from the bottom. My grandmother taught me that. She had a whole sunroom full of them, just blooming year-round. She always used to say that plants know when you're paying attention to them. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Convincing me? No, absolutely not. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or clerical errors, I know they’re keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. You can't just explain away a pattern like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be mistaken about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I'm not mistaken. Look, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. It's undeniable. My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she borrowed my hedge clippers three weeks ago and still hasn't returned them. I've called her twice. She has those overgrown azalea bushes right along her property line, and they just look terrible if you don't keep them trimmed. Azaleas need a good pruning right after they finish blooming in the spring, otherwise they get all leggy and bare at the bottom. I learned that the hard way with the ones I planted in my front yard back when I first bought my house. That was in 1998, right before the big ice storm that knocked out power for five days. I had to sleep in my winter coat and heat up cans of soup over a little camping stove I found in the basement. The camping stove belonged to my ex-husband, actually. He used to love going up to the Adirondacks to fish, but I never saw the appeal of sitting in a damp boat at five in the morning.\n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Mistaken? No, absolutely not. Even when everyone says it's just mistakes or coincidences, I know the truth. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's perfectly clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about these people trying to keep information from you when you're not directly dealing with your sister's appointments or bills?\n\nPatient: Think about them all the time? Well, people might think I'm too focused on this whole situation, but no. Outside of dealing with my sister's appointments and trying to get straight answers from these people, I still keep my home in perfect order, I pay my bills, and I do my job. \n\nSpeaking of doing my job, we had an absolute disaster at the office on Tuesday. One of the new administrative assistants—sweet girl, but absolutely no common sense—tried to replace the toner in the main copier without reading the manual first. Toner exploded everywhere. It looked like a coal mine in the breakroom! And it got all over my favorite cream-colored silk blouse. You can't just wash silk, you know. You have to take it to a specialty dry cleaner. I use this place downtown, run by a lovely couple, but they just raised their prices again. It's getting ridiculous, really, but what can you do? If you try to hand-wash silk yourself, it loses that beautiful sheen and just looks like cheap polyester. I bought that blouse for my niece's college graduation back in 2019. She got her degree in communications, whatever that means nowadays. She wants to do public relations, but honestly, half the time she's just staring at her phone making those little videos. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, about thinking about it when I'm not dealing with it. No. Like I said, I’m not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I go to work, I manage my staff, I keep my house clean. I just know what's going on with my sister's care, and I won't let them sweep it under the rug.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself questioning your own thoughts about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Question my own thoughts? No, why would I? I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It's not something I'm making up in my head. My sister’s appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of bills showing up wrong, the electric company tried to charge me double last month! They said it was an estimated reading because the meter reader couldn't get into the yard. But my gate is never locked. I have one of those wrought iron gates, very classic looking. I had it installed after the neighborhood association complained about my old wooden fence leaning too far over the sidewalk. Some people just have nothing better to do than measure the angle of a fence, I swear. The woman who heads the committee, Shirley, she walks her little Pomeranian past my house every morning at exactly 7:15. You can set your watch by it. She always wears these bright pink tracksuits. Who wears bright pink at seven in the morning? It's just entirely too loud for that time of day. I prefer a nice muted beige or a soft navy. Navy is so professional, don't you think? It commands respect without being aggressive like red. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Questioning myself? No, no. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or clerical errors, I know exactly what's happening. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any circumstance under which you would consider the possibility that these events are not part of a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Consider the possibility? No, why would I do that when the proof is right in front of me? I've been seeing the exact same pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, the plumber I called last week still hasn't returned my message. I have a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom, and it's driving me absolutely up the wall. Drip, drip, drip. It's one of those antique brass fixtures, too, so you can't just go to the hardware store and buy a standard washer for it. You have to order the parts special. I found them online, but the shipping takes forever. I hate ordering things online. The delivery drivers always leave the packages right on the edge of the porch where anyone walking by could just snatch them up. Last Christmas, someone stole a fruitcake I had ordered for my uncle. Not that he would have eaten it anyway—he has terrible dentures and can't chew anything harder than oatmeal. He got those dentures fitted back in 2012, and he's complained about them every single day since. He always says they make him look like a horse when he smiles. \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Circumstances? No, there are no circumstances where I'd think it's just mistakes or coincidences. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you ever consider seeking a second opinion about what's happening with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Second opinion? From who? Another doctor who's just going to cover for the rest of them? I don't need a second opinion because I already know what's happening. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of doctors covering for each other, my dentist tried to refer me to an endodontist last month for a root canal. He said my molar was cracked, but I don't feel any pain at all. None. I think they just send patients back and forth to drum up business for each other. It's like those mechanics who tell you that you need new brake pads when you just went in for an oil change. I take my car to a guy named Sal over on 4th Street. He's honest, mostly, but his waiting room smells strongly of old cigars and wet dog. He has this massive mastiff that just sleeps right in the middle of the floor, and you have to step over him to get to the counter. The dog's name is Brutus, which is so cliché, don't you think? I always thought if I got a dog, I'd name it something elegant, like Winston or maybe Oliver. But I don't have the time for a pet. They tie you down too much. You can't just take a weekend trip to the coast if you have to worry about who is going to feed the dog. The coast is lovely this time of year, though. The sea air is so bracing. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? A second opinion? No, I don't need one. Even when everyone—the nurses, the clinic manager, everyone—says it’s just mistakes, I know better. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's obvious.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could happen or anything someone could tell you that would make you change your mind about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? What could possibly happen to change my mind when the facts are right there? I've been seeing this exact pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without a word to me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of not calling back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she never calls me back either. I called her three times last week to ask if she’d seen anyone lingering around my sister’s porch, because I noticed the potted geraniums were moved. Geraniums are so finicky, you know. If they don't get exactly the right amount of morning sun, they just wither up. I used to have a whole row of them on my balcony when I lived in the city, back when I was managing that big insurance office downtown. I oversaw a team of twenty administrative assistants. Twenty! You really have to know how to manage people to keep a place like that running. Most of them were fresh out of college and didn't know the first thing about filing or proper phone etiquette. I had to write a whole manual on how to answer the phone. \"Good morning, Smith and Associates, how may I direct your call?\" It's not hard, but you'd be surprised. One girl, Brenda, she used to chew gum while she answered the phones. Smacked it right in the receiver. I had to let her go after a month. You just can't have that kind of unprofessionalism when you're dealing with important clients. \n\nBut what were you asking? Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the billing department—they all try to tell me it's just mistakes or clerical errors. But I know better. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. Nothing is going to change my mind because I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would change your belief about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could they possibly have? I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of evidence, I was watching this true crime documentary last night on cable. They were talking about forensic evidence, DNA and all that. It's fascinating how they can solve a crime from thirty years ago just from a single strand of hair. I always thought I'd be good at that kind of work, paying attention to all the little details. You really need an eye for detail. Like when I was auditing the expense reports at the insurance firm. I caught one of the junior executives trying to expense a $400 dinner at a steakhouse as a \"client meeting\" when it was clearly his anniversary dinner. He had ordered a bottle of champagne! Who orders champagne at a client dinner on a Tuesday? I flagged it immediately. My supervisor, Mr. Henderson, was so impressed. Mr. Henderson was a good man, but he wore these terrible knit ties that were always slightly crooked. I wanted to reach across his desk and straighten them every time we had a meeting. He retired to Florida a few years ago. Bought a condo in Boca Raton. I hear the humidity down there is just awful for your joints. My knees ache just thinking about it. \n\nBut what were you asking? Evidence? No, there's no evidence that would change my mind. Even when everyone—the clinic manager, the doctors, the billing staff—even when they all look me in the eye and say it's just administrative mistakes, I know what's really going on. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's clear as day.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything, no matter how small, that could make you doubt your belief that people are intentionally keeping information from you?\n\nPatient: Doubt it? Doctor, I feel like a broken record today. Like I keep saying, I have watched this exact pattern for months. The appointments getting shuffled around behind my back, the billing errors, the staff suddenly forgetting how to dial my phone number... everyone keeps telling me they're just innocent mistakes until they're blue in the face, but I know exactly what's going on. They are deliberately keeping information from me to push me out of the decisions about my sister's care. \n\nSpeaking of blue in the face, that reminds me of this horrible blue carpeting corporate made us install at the insurance office back in 2004. They claimed it would create a \"calming work environment,\" but it was this cheap, awful shag material. It was a complete nightmare to keep clean. The girls' rolling chairs would just sink right into it. I had to order special plastic floor mats for all twenty of my administrative assistants just so they could roll from their desks to the filing cabinets without getting stuck. \n\nAnd getting those mats approved by the purchasing department? Don't even get me started. The purchasing manager, Greg, guarded the office supply budget like it was his own personal fortune. He would literally count the boxes of paperclips. I told him once, \"Greg, we are a major firm, we can afford the heavy-duty binder clips.\" He was a very strange man. He drove this tiny little yellow car that looked like a lemon on wheels. I actually ran into him at the grocery store once on a Sunday, and he had six bags of frozen peas in his cart. Just peas. Who eats that many frozen peas? It’s terrible for your digestion to eat that much starch. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? Something small? No, absolutely nothing is going to make me doubt it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Consider it? Doctor, we've been over this. I am here because for months now, I have been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved around, the bills show up completely wrong, people constantly "forget" to call me back. I don't need to consider anything else because it's right in front of me.

Filing her nails! At the front desk! I couldn't believe it. I almost marched up there and gave her a piece of my mind. When I was managing the administrative staff at the insurance firm, if I ever caught one of my girls doing her nails at her desk, she would have been written up immediately. We had a very strict dress and grooming code. You had to look professional. Pantyhose were mandatory, even in the summer. It was hot, yes, but it looked sharp. My favorite pair of heels back then were these navy blue pumps. They had just the right size heel—not too high, so you could walk around the office, but high enough to look authoritative. I bought them at a little boutique downtown that went out of business years ago. Such a shame. They had the most wonderful Italian leather goods. I bought a matching navy blue handbag there that I carried for a decade. The clasp finally broke, and I tried to get it fixed by this cobbler on 4th Street, but he said the mechanism was too old. 

What were we talking about? Oh, right. Considering it's not true. No, absolutely not. Even when everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the whole clinic staff—tries to tell me it's just mistakes, I know better. They are deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. I won't be convinced otherwise.

Persuaded? Doctor, honestly, are we speaking the same language today? I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall. How many times do I have to say it? I have been watching this exact same pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved around, the bills show up completely wrong, the staff magically "forget" to call me back. It smelled like pine needles and cheap peppermint. It was completely overwhelming. My lead file clerk, Brenda, had to go home early twice because the smell gave her such terrible migraines. Poor Brenda. She was a wonderful worker, very organized, but she had a very sensitive constitution. She couldn't even eat the catered sandwiches at the office holiday party if they had too much mayonnaise on them. 

We always ordered from this little deli on 5th Avenue for the holiday lunches. They made the most wonderful pimento cheese. They served it on this thick, marbled rye bread. My mother used to make pimento cheese for Sunday picnics when my sister and I were little girls. We'd go down to the lake and sit on this old tartan blanket... she had this wicker picnic basket that squeaked every time you opened the lid. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Persuaded. Right. No, absolutely not. You can't persuade me otherwise. I know what I see. No amount of talking is going to convince me it's just administrative errors. I know they're trying to push me out.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m here because... honestly, for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. I went to the store over by the new shopping center—have you seen the traffic over there lately? It is an absolute nightmare since they put in that new intersection. You sit through three lights just to turn left. And the young man working at the counter barely even looked up from his tablet to help me. I supervise an entire office of administrative staff, and let me tell you, if one of my girls ignored someone like that, I’d have them on a performance plan by Friday afternoon. People just don't take pride in their work anymore, it's all just rushing through things. \n\nBut anyway, everyone keeps telling me, \"Oh, it’s just clerical mistakes, people are just busy.\" But I know what's really going on. I know they’re deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are working together to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, exactly. Like I was just saying, I know they are keeping information from me. It's not just a coincidence, they are absolutely coordinating this to push me out of the decisions. You don't just have bills show up wrong and appointments moved month after month by accident. \n\nTake the billing department at my sister's clinic, for instance. I called them last week, and the woman on the phone—she had this awful, loud chewing habit. Smacking her gum right in my ear! I couldn't believe it. I tell my administrative staff, \"If I ever catch you chewing gum on a call, you'll be packing your desk.\" It's basic professional etiquette. We actually hosted a whole seminar on phone etiquette for my team just last quarter. Brought in a lovely speaker from downtown, catered a nice Panera lunch for everyone, the turkey thick-cut sandwiches... \n\nBut anyway, yes. The gum-chewing woman, the scheduling people... they are all doing it on purpose to keep me in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any other explanation for these events besides people intentionally trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? No, absolutely not. I mean, everyone keeps telling me, \"Oh, it’s just mistakes, the clinic is just understaffed.\" But I know what's really going on. I know they are keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. You don't just have bills show up wrong and appointments moved for months on end by accident. \n\nSpeaking of accidents, I had a girl at my office—Melissa, sweet girl, but just completely disorganized—and she tried to tell me last week that she \"accidentally\" deleted the entire third-quarter expense report. Just wiped it out! Do you know how hard it is to recover files from that new cloud system corporate forced us to switch to in January? It is a total nightmare. The IT guy, David, had to come down and sit at her desk for three hours to fix it. \n\nAnd he brought his dog into the office that day! A little golden doodle. Very cute, don't get me wrong, but completely distracting for the front desk staff. I had to draft a whole new memo about pets in the workplace before I even left for the day. We can't just have animals running around where we file confidential paperwork, it's a liability. \n\nBut anyway... no. There is no other explanation. I've seen the pattern for months, and they are definitely doing all of this to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about these people trying to keep information from you most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? Well, people might think I’m a little too focused on this whole situation, but I am certainly not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I have a very demanding job, and I still go in every day and do it well. I keep my house in perfect order, I pay my bills on time... \n\nActually, speaking of bills, I just had to call the utility company on Monday because they tried to double-charge me for the municipal sewer fee. I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Can you believe that? Forty-five minutes just listening to that awful, repetitive elevator music. It sounded like a cheap synthesizer from the 1980s. I told my ex-husband once—we divorced back in '08, thank goodness—he used to play the keyboard, and he always played those exact same tinny chords. It drove me absolutely up the wall. He would sit in the garage for hours with that thing instead of helping me put down the new mulch in the front yard. I ended up having to buy eighteen bags of cedar mulch from Home Depot and haul them all to the flowerbeds myself. My lower back hasn't been the same since, let me tell you. I still have to see a chiropractor twice a month.\n\nBut anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, if I think about them all the time. No, outside of dealing with my sister’s appointments and just trying to get a straight answer out of those schedulers, I function perfectly normally. I have a very full schedule, so I'm not just sitting around obsessing all day, even if I know exactly what they're trying to pull.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you personally, or is it just about keeping information from you?\n\nPatient: Harm me personally? No, I don't think they're trying to hurt me, like physically or anything like that. It's really just about keeping the information from me and pushing me out of the loop with my sister's care. They just want me out of the way. \n\nSpeaking of being out of the way, I had to completely rearrange the breakroom at the office yesterday because people kept leaving their dirty coffee mugs right in the sink instead of putting them in the dishwasher. It’s not that hard! The dishwasher is literally two feet to the left. I had to type up a very clear, bolded sign and tape it right above the faucet. You know, I used to use that heavy-duty packing tape for signs, but it leaves this awful sticky residue on the wall. I found this wonderful double-sided mounting tape at Staples last week. It holds up beautifully and peels right off. I bought three rolls just to have in my supply closet. The cashier at Staples was so slow, though. He had to call a manager over just to process a simple coupon I had from their email newsletter. I stood there for ten minutes. I could have audited a whole stack of invoices in the time it took him to scan a barcode. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, they aren't trying to harm me. He had to call a manager over just to process a simple coupon I had from their email newsletter. I stood there for ten minutes. I could have audited a whole stack of invoices in the time it took him to scan a barcode. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, they aren't trying to harm me. They just want me in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Wrong? No. I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It's clear as day. My sister’s appointments get moved without my permission, bills show up completely wrong, and people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, my mechanic has had my Subaru since Tuesday for a simple brake pad replacement. He said he'd call me by noon yesterday, and nothing! I finally had to call him myself at four o'clock, and he tried to tell me they were waiting on a part to be delivered. A brake pad! For a 2018 Outback! They are the most common cars on the road, especially around here. You can't throw a rock without hitting a silver Subaru Outback. I bought mine in silver specifically because it doesn't show the salt spray in the winter. They use so much road salt in this county, it's terrible for the undercarriage. I have to take it to the car wash on Main Street at least once a week from December to March. The guy who runs the wash, Hector, he always tries to upsell me on the premium wax treatment. I tell him, \"Hector, I just want the salt off, I don't need a showroom shine.\" He's a nice man, though. His daughter just graduated from the state college with a degree in communications. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, about being wrong. No, even when everyone tells me it’s just mistakes or short-staffing, I know the truth. I know they’re deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's not a mistake, it's a strategy.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind if you found out that the mistakes with your sister's appointments and bills were actually due to clerical errors and not a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Because I'm here because for months now, I've been seeing the exact same pattern. It's not just one or two little clerical errors. My sister's appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of bills showing up wrong, I just got my credit card statement yesterday and there was a charge from a restaurant I haven't been to in three years. Three years! It was that little Italian place over on 4th Street, Mario's. I stopped going there because they changed their marinara sauce recipe. It used to be so rich and garlicky, and then suddenly it tasted like it came straight out of a tin can. I asked the waiter about it—he was a young kid, couldn't have been more than nineteen, had his hair slicked back with way too much gel—and he swore nothing changed. But I know my palate. I used to cook all the time before I got promoted to office supervisor. I made a lasagna that won first prize at the church potluck back in 2012. I used three different kinds of cheese and homemade pasta. Making pasta from scratch takes forever, though. You have to let the dough rest for at least thirty minutes, and then rolling it out is an absolute workout for your shoulders. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, about changing my mind. No. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or short-staffing, I know the truth. I know they’re deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. You don't just have this many \"clerical errors\" by coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that these events are not part of a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Convince me? No, I don't think so. I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It’s right there in front of everyone. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting things, I was at the grocery store this morning—the big one over on Elm, not the little market by my house—and I completely forgot my reusable bags. I had them sitting right by the front door, right next to the umbrella stand. I bought that umbrella stand at a flea market out in Lancaster County about ten years ago. It’s real brass, very heavy. You don't see craftsmanship like that anymore. Everything now is just cheap plastic from overseas. I tried to buy a new watering can for my African violets last week, and the handle snapped off the very first time I filled it. African violets are so finicky, you know. You can't get water on their leaves or they rot. You have to water them from the bottom. My grandmother taught me that. She had a whole sunroom full of them, just blooming year-round. She always used to say that plants know when you're paying attention to them. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Convincing me? No, absolutely not. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or clerical errors, I know they’re keeping information from me. I know they're trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. You can't just explain away a pattern like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be mistaken about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I'm not mistaken. Look, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. It's undeniable. My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she borrowed my hedge clippers three weeks ago and still hasn't returned them. I've called her twice. She has those overgrown azalea bushes right along her property line, and they just look terrible if you don't keep them trimmed. Azaleas need a good pruning right after they finish blooming in the spring, otherwise they get all leggy and bare at the bottom. I learned that the hard way with the ones I planted in my front yard back when I first bought my house. That was in 1998, right before the big ice storm that knocked out power for five days. I had to sleep in my winter coat and heat up cans of soup over a little camping stove I found in the basement. The camping stove belonged to my ex-husband, actually. He used to love going up to the Adirondacks to fish, but I never saw the appeal of sitting in a damp boat at five in the morning.\n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Mistaken? No, absolutely not. Even when everyone says it's just mistakes or coincidences, I know the truth. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's perfectly clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about these people trying to keep information from you when you're not directly dealing with your sister's appointments or bills?\n\nPatient: Think about them all the time? Well, people might think I'm too focused on this whole situation, but no. Outside of dealing with my sister's appointments and trying to get straight answers from these people, I still keep my home in perfect order, I pay my bills, and I do my job. \n\nSpeaking of doing my job, we had an absolute disaster at the office on Tuesday. One of the new administrative assistants—sweet girl, but absolutely no common sense—tried to replace the toner in the main copier without reading the manual first. Toner exploded everywhere. It looked like a coal mine in the breakroom! And it got all over my favorite cream-colored silk blouse. You can't just wash silk, you know. You have to take it to a specialty dry cleaner. I use this place downtown, run by a lovely couple, but they just raised their prices again. It's getting ridiculous, really, but what can you do? If you try to hand-wash silk yourself, it loses that beautiful sheen and just looks like cheap polyester. I bought that blouse for my niece's college graduation back in 2019. She got her degree in communications, whatever that means nowadays. She wants to do public relations, but honestly, half the time she's just staring at her phone making those little videos. \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, about thinking about it when I'm not dealing with it. No. Like I said, I’m not falling apart or acting strangely in other parts of my life. I go to work, I manage my staff, I keep my house clean. I just know what's going on with my sister's care, and I won't let them sweep it under the rug.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself questioning your own thoughts about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Question my own thoughts? No, why would I? I mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. It's not something I'm making up in my head. My sister’s appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of bills showing up wrong, the electric company tried to charge me double last month! They said it was an estimated reading because the meter reader couldn't get into the yard. But my gate is never locked. I have one of those wrought iron gates, very classic looking. I had it installed after the neighborhood association complained about my old wooden fence leaning too far over the sidewalk. Some people just have nothing better to do than measure the angle of a fence, I swear. The woman who heads the committee, Shirley, she walks her little Pomeranian past my house every morning at exactly 7:15. You can set your watch by it. She always wears these bright pink tracksuits. Who wears bright pink at seven in the morning? It's just entirely too loud for that time of day. I prefer a nice muted beige or a soft navy. Navy is so professional, don't you think? It commands respect without being aggressive like red. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Questioning myself? No, no. Even when everyone says it’s just mistakes or clerical errors, I know exactly what's happening. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. It's plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any circumstance under which you would consider the possibility that these events are not part of a deliberate attempt to keep you in the dark?\n\nPatient: Consider the possibility? No, why would I do that when the proof is right in front of me? I've been seeing the exact same pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without anyone telling me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of forgetting to call back, the plumber I called last week still hasn't returned my message. I have a leaky faucet in the guest bathroom, and it's driving me absolutely up the wall. Drip, drip, drip. It's one of those antique brass fixtures, too, so you can't just go to the hardware store and buy a standard washer for it. You have to order the parts special. I found them online, but the shipping takes forever. I hate ordering things online. The delivery drivers always leave the packages right on the edge of the porch where anyone walking by could just snatch them up. Last Christmas, someone stole a fruitcake I had ordered for my uncle. Not that he would have eaten it anyway—he has terrible dentures and can't chew anything harder than oatmeal. He got those dentures fitted back in 2012, and he's complained about them every single day since. He always says they make him look like a horse when he smiles. \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Circumstances? No, there are no circumstances where I'd think it's just mistakes or coincidences. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions regarding my sister. I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Would you ever consider seeking a second opinion about what's happening with your sister's care?\n\nPatient: Second opinion? From who? Another doctor who's just going to cover for the rest of them? I don't need a second opinion because I already know what's happening. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of doctors covering for each other, my dentist tried to refer me to an endodontist last month for a root canal. He said my molar was cracked, but I don't feel any pain at all. None. I think they just send patients back and forth to drum up business for each other. It's like those mechanics who tell you that you need new brake pads when you just went in for an oil change. I take my car to a guy named Sal over on 4th Street. He's honest, mostly, but his waiting room smells strongly of old cigars and wet dog. He has this massive mastiff that just sleeps right in the middle of the floor, and you have to step over him to get to the counter. The dog's name is Brutus, which is so cliché, don't you think? I always thought if I got a dog, I'd name it something elegant, like Winston or maybe Oliver. But I don't have the time for a pet. They tie you down too much. You can't just take a weekend trip to the coast if you have to worry about who is going to feed the dog. The coast is lovely this time of year, though. The sea air is so bracing. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? A second opinion? No, I don't need one. Even when everyone—the nurses, the clinic manager, everyone—says it’s just mistakes, I know better. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's obvious.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could happen or anything someone could tell you that would make you change your mind about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Change my mind? What could possibly happen to change my mind when the facts are right there? I've been seeing this exact pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved without a word to me, the bills show up wrong, people conveniently forget to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of not calling back, my sister's neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she never calls me back either. I called her three times last week to ask if she’d seen anyone lingering around my sister’s porch, because I noticed the potted geraniums were moved. Geraniums are so finicky, you know. If they don't get exactly the right amount of morning sun, they just wither up. I used to have a whole row of them on my balcony when I lived in the city, back when I was managing that big insurance office downtown. I oversaw a team of twenty administrative assistants. Twenty! You really have to know how to manage people to keep a place like that running. Most of them were fresh out of college and didn't know the first thing about filing or proper phone etiquette. I had to write a whole manual on how to answer the phone. \"Good morning, Smith and Associates, how may I direct your call?\" It's not hard, but you'd be surprised. One girl, Brenda, she used to chew gum while she answered the phones. Smacked it right in the receiver. I had to let her go after a month. You just can't have that kind of unprofessionalism when you're dealing with important clients. \n\nBut what were you asking? Change my mind? No, absolutely not. Everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the billing department—they all try to tell me it's just mistakes or clerical errors. But I know better. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. Nothing is going to change my mind because I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would change your belief about people trying to keep information from you?\n\nPatient: Evidence? What kind of evidence could they possibly have? I mean, I'm here because for months now I've been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved without my knowledge, the bills show up wrong, people \"forget\" to call me back. \n\nSpeaking of evidence, I was watching this true crime documentary last night on cable. They were talking about forensic evidence, DNA and all that. It's fascinating how they can solve a crime from thirty years ago just from a single strand of hair. I always thought I'd be good at that kind of work, paying attention to all the little details. You really need an eye for detail. Like when I was auditing the expense reports at the insurance firm. I caught one of the junior executives trying to expense a $400 dinner at a steakhouse as a \"client meeting\" when it was clearly his anniversary dinner. He had ordered a bottle of champagne! Who orders champagne at a client dinner on a Tuesday? I flagged it immediately. My supervisor, Mr. Henderson, was so impressed. Mr. Henderson was a good man, but he wore these terrible knit ties that were always slightly crooked. I wanted to reach across his desk and straighten them every time we had a meeting. He retired to Florida a few years ago. Bought a condo in Boca Raton. I hear the humidity down there is just awful for your joints. My knees ache just thinking about it. \n\nBut what were you asking? Evidence? No, there's no evidence that would change my mind. Even when everyone—the clinic manager, the doctors, the billing staff—even when they all look me in the eye and say it's just administrative mistakes, I know what's really going on. I know they're keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. It's clear as day.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything, no matter how small, that could make you doubt your belief that people are intentionally keeping information from you?\n\nPatient: Doubt it? Doctor, I feel like a broken record today. Like I keep saying, I have watched this exact pattern for months. The appointments getting shuffled around behind my back, the billing errors, the staff suddenly forgetting how to dial my phone number... everyone keeps telling me they're just innocent mistakes until they're blue in the face, but I know exactly what's going on. They are deliberately keeping information from me to push me out of the decisions about my sister's care. \n\nSpeaking of blue in the face, that reminds me of this horrible blue carpeting corporate made us install at the insurance office back in 2004. They claimed it would create a \"calming work environment,\" but it was this cheap, awful shag material. It was a complete nightmare to keep clean. The girls' rolling chairs would just sink right into it. I had to order special plastic floor mats for all twenty of my administrative assistants just so they could roll from their desks to the filing cabinets without getting stuck. \n\nAnd getting those mats approved by the purchasing department? Don't even get me started. The purchasing manager, Greg, guarded the office supply budget like it was his own personal fortune. He would literally count the boxes of paperclips. I told him once, \"Greg, we are a major firm, we can afford the heavy-duty binder clips.\" He was a very strange man. He drove this tiny little yellow car that looked like a lemon on wheels. I actually ran into him at the grocery store once on a Sunday, and he had six bags of frozen peas in his cart. Just peas. Who eats that many frozen peas? It’s terrible for your digestion to eat that much starch. \n\nBut... wait, what did you ask me? Something small? No, absolutely nothing is going to make me doubt it. I know they're keeping me in the dark about my sister. It's not just mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything at all that could make you consider that your beliefs about people keeping information from you might not be true?\n\nPatient: Consider it? Doctor, we've been over this. I am here because for months now, I have been seeing the exact same pattern. My sister's appointments get moved around, the bills show up completely wrong, people constantly \"forget\" to call me back. I don't need to consider anything else because it's right in front of me.\n\nSpeaking of things in front of me, did I tell you about the new receptionist at my dentist's office? She sits right there at the front desk, plain as day, and just ignores the phone when it rings. I was sitting in the waiting room last Tuesday—or was it Wednesday? No, Tuesday, because I had my bridge club in the afternoon. Anyway, the phone rang five times, and she just kept filing her nails. Filing her nails! At the front desk! I couldn't believe it. I almost marched up there and gave her a piece of my mind. When I was managing the administrative staff at the insurance firm, if I ever caught one of my girls doing her nails at her desk, she would have been written up immediately. We had a very strict dress and grooming code. You had to look professional. Pantyhose were mandatory, even in the summer. It was hot, yes, but it looked sharp. My favorite pair of heels back then were these navy blue pumps. They had just the right size heel—not too high, so you could walk around the office, but high enough to look authoritative. I bought them at a little boutique downtown that went out of business years ago. Such a shame. They had the most wonderful Italian leather goods. I bought a matching navy blue handbag there that I carried for a decade. The clasp finally broke, and I tried to get it fixed by this cobbler on 4th Street, but he said the mechanism was too old. \n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Considering it's not true. No, absolutely not. Even when everyone—the nurses, the doctors, the whole clinic staff—tries to tell me it's just mistakes, I know better. They are deliberately keeping information from me and trying to push me out of the decisions about my sister. I won't be convinced otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever be persuaded that your beliefs about people keeping information from you are incorrect?\n\nPatient: Persuaded? Doctor, honestly, are we speaking the same language today? I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall. How many times do I have to say it? I have been watching this exact same pattern for months now. My sister's appointments get moved around, the bills show up completely wrong, the staff magically \"forget\" to call me back. Everyone keeps telling me they're just mistakes, but I know what's going on. They are deliberately keeping information from me to push me out of the decisions about her care. \n\nPersuaded... you know, it reminds me of this awful salesman who used to come into the insurance firm. Martin, his name was. He was always trying to persuade me to upgrade our copy machines to these massive, expensive color units. He would come in with these glossy brochures and this incredibly strong cologne. It smelled like pine needles and cheap peppermint. It was completely overwhelming. My lead file clerk, Brenda, had to go home early twice because the smell gave her such terrible migraines. Poor Brenda. She was a wonderful worker, very organized, but she had a very sensitive constitution. She couldn't even eat the catered sandwiches at the office holiday party if they had too much mayonnaise on them. \n\nWe always ordered from this little deli on 5th Avenue for the holiday lunches. They made the most wonderful pimento cheese. They served it on this thick, marbled rye bread. My mother used to make pimento cheese for Sunday picnics when my sister and I were little girls. We'd go down to the lake and sit on this old tartan blanket... she had this wicker picnic basket that squeaked every time you opened the lid. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Persuaded. Right. No, absolutely not. You can't persuade me otherwise. I know what I see. No amount of talking is going to convince me it's just administrative errors. I know they're trying to push me out."
